- 2026-09-12 · V4 DPV is not available on this site license: the DPV app starts (`-batch`, system terminfo, bash-as-/bin/sh namespace), compiles both RTL sides and composes them, but the solver cannot check out `VC-FORMAL-DPV-BASE-SH` (FlexNet status -5, no such feature); `src/equiv/dpv.py` reports `unavailable` and never a proof. Consequence (spec 03 §5): candidates that SEQ leaves inconclusive stay inconclusive (out of the population, reported per class); a Yosys `equiv_*` fallback through flow/equiv.py (proven / not_proven, class (a) and small sequential designs) may be added after the Phase 2 SEQ pilot if the inconclusive rate warrants it, and would be stated in the paper · CLAUDE.md rule 8, spec 03 §1/§5 · config `tools.vcformal.dpv_app_ok: false`, src/equiv/dpv.py, tests/test_dpv_eda.py · Claude Code (license fact), fallback choice pending human confirmation at G0
- 2026-09-12 · Correction of the previous entry: V4 DPV IS usable. The FlexNet -5 for `VC-FORMAL-DPV-BASE-SH` is the tool's first attempt; it then checks out `VC-FORMAL-DPV-ELITE-SH` (and Hector). With `solveNB -ual ual -maxtime N p1; proofwait p1` the 8-bit multiplier rewrite is proven and the injected bug falsified in ~50 s each (`tests/test_dpv_eda.py`). `src/equiv/dpv.py` reports `unavailable` only when no DPV feature at all is checked out; the stack runs V4 after an inconclusive SEQ on combinational modules (no clock port); clocked datapaths wait for the Phase 2 pilot (phase mapping) · spec 03 §1 · config `tools.vcformal.dpv_app_ok: true`, src/equiv/dpv.py, src/equiv/stack.py · Claude Code

- 2026-09-12 · V4 (DPV) scope and guardrails (human decision, verbatim policy): V4 is enabled only for combinational modules (no clock port) and only after an inconclusive V3. Guardrail 1: V3's counterexample takes precedence: a SEQ `falsified` is final, V4 is not run and can never overturn it. Guardrail 2: a V4 `proven` is accepted only if (i) the lemmas cover every output of the module, (ii) the transaction carries no `assume` constraint, and (iii) a non-vacuity check for that module passed: at least one deliberately injected-error variant (spec 03 §3 mutants, generated by `src/equiv/mutants.py`, confirmed functionally different by lock-step simulation) is `falsified` by DPV; the record keeps `v4_status` separately and a `proven_by = seq | dpv` field; the paper reports how many proofs come from DPV. Guardrail 3: automation for clocked datapaths is decided after the Phase 2 SEQ pilot, which records (a) how many SEQ-inconclusive candidates are clocked arithmetic modules, (b) whether the per-output offsets measured by V2 are constant across all random runs, (c) whether the module has recognisable start/valid and done/valid signals; only modules meeting all three qualify for automatic phase derivation with the lemma "at phase 1 both starts are 1 and the inputs are equal ⇒ spec.out(k1) == impl.out(k2)" plus a cover proving done is reachable at k1/k2 (anti-vacuity); the rest stay inconclusive · reports/phase0.md question 6 · docs/spec/03-equivalence.md, docs/PLAN.md 2.4, src/equiv/stack.py, src/equiv/dpv.py, src/equiv/mutants.py, src/db/schema.sql (`candidates.proven_by`) · human
- 2026-09-12 · Ladder check requested at G0 (facts measured on DC W-2024.09-SP5-3, wire-load mode, nangate45; `reports/phase0.md` addendum, `reports/data/phase0_ladder_probe_*.json`): (1) `-timing_high_effort_script` and `-area_high_effort_script` are documented as "kept for backward compatibility, ignored for optimization" and E2t is bit-identical to E2 on all four probe designs; (2) the replacement strategy variable `compile_timing_high_effort` is not an application variable in wire-load mode ("not supported in WLM mode, ignoring", OPT-1346) and only exists in topographical mode, `compile_high_effort_area` is DC NXT only; so no timing-high-effort capability exists in the WLM ladder of this version; (3) `-retime` acts on near-critical pipelined designs (multi_pipe_8bit, adder_pipe_64bit: register counts and area change) and not on combinational or slack-rich designs; (4) `-gate_clock` is the largest E3→E4 effect where enable registers exist (adder_pipe_64bit −6% area, 4 ICGs); (5) `compile` with DesignWare Foundation (current E1) differs from `compile` with the standard synthetic library only (E1s) by 3–6% on every design, in both directions; (6) H5 differs from E2/E4 on 3 of 4 designs. Proposed redefinition (pending human decision): E1 = `compile` + standard.sldb only; E2 = `compile_ultra` (DesignWare Foundation auto-added, datapath extraction, boundary optimisation, auto-ungroup, area strategy); E3 = `compile_ultra -retime`; E4 = `compile_ultra -retime -gate_clock`; single-flag attribution rungs E1d = `compile` + dw_foundation (DesignWare architecture), E2r, E2g; E2t dropped; H3 = E4 + `-spg` (topographical) may additionally enable `compile_timing_high_effort` as the physical-aware full-effort configuration · PROPOSAL §2, spec 01 §1 · config/experiments.yaml (after the decision) · Claude Code (facts), human (definition)
- 2026-09-12 · Ladder adopted as proposed (human): E1 = `compile` with the standard synthetic library only, E1d = `compile` + DesignWare Foundation (official attribution rung), E2 = `compile_ultra`, E3 = `compile_ultra -retime`, E4 = `compile_ultra -retime -gate_clock` (main scoring, wire-load mode), E2r alias of E3, E2g = `compile_ultra -gate_clock`, E2t dropped. Supplements: (1) E4 stays the wire-load-mode main scoring configuration and does not move to topographical mode: wire-load to topographical is a mode switch, not an added capability, and would break the attribution chain at the top rung; H3 = E4 + `-spg` in topographical mode with `compile_timing_high_effort` is the physical-aware full-effort hidden configuration; Phase 2 measures t_H3 / t_E4 and the E4-vs-H3 agreement rate on the noise-floor set, and G3 decides whether the main scoring configuration changes; paper wording for E4: "wire-load-mode full-effort synthesis (compile_ultra + retiming + clock gating); this version's timing-high-effort strategy exists only in topographical mode and is covered by the hidden configuration H3". (2) The synthetic library is a capability with a non-monotone effect (DesignWare made `compile` worse on the divider): it goes into the map and links to the permanent-absorption definition. (3) A hierarchy attribution rung E2u = `compile_ultra -no_autoungroup` is added only if the Phase 4 map shows many class-(b) candidates absorbed at E2. (4) Implementation: the ignored flag removed from every command string, `exp1.configs` uses E1d / E2r / E2g, every DC configuration carries `synlib`, fixtures regenerated and the determinism test rerun, PROPOSAL §2 / §3.2 (CktEvo row: "equivalent to E3 on DC W-2024.09; effect on their version unknown") / §6.3 and spec 01 §1 synced · reports/phase0.md addendum · config/experiments.yaml, docs/PROPOSAL.md, docs/spec/01-eval-service.md, docs/PLAN.md, src/eval/service.py · human
- 2026-09-12 · Y caliber stays the proposal's definition (option a) (human). Supplements: (1) Ycoevo (COEVO's exact Yosys script and OpenSTA setting: `flatten; opt -full`, zero IO delays, `set_max_delay` in→out) is a mandatory supplementary Exp1 column: exact comparability with the group's prior work and evidence that Yosys settings are not comparable among themselves; (2) `dfflibmap` is recorded as a correctness fix, not a caliber change, and supplies a footnote that Yosys `stat` area numbers in the literature rarely state whether flip-flops were mapped; (3) COEVO's original numbers are cited with a footnote on the script and IO-delay differences · PROPOSAL §2, §10 · config `configs.Ycoevo`, src/eval/yosys.py · human

- 2026-09-12 · Clock and reset identification by use: the inventory runs Yosys `proc; flatten` and takes as clock ports the inputs that drive flip-flop clock pins (memory clocks included) and as asynchronous reset the input on `$adff` ARST with its polarity; names are only a fallback for synchronous resets. Name matching alone mislabelled 29 CktEvo modules (WB_CLK_I, MTxClk, MRxClk) and the RTLLM two-clock designs as combinational · PLAN 1.2 · src/designs/yosys_probe.py, src/designs/inventory.py, tests/test_designs_yosys.py · Claude Code

- 2026-09-12 · Set assignment rules (config `design_sets`): CktEvo set = round-robin over the repositories (alphabetical), each repository's eligible pool modules ordered by size (loc descending), at most `max_per_repo` = 5 per repository, until `target_count` = 30; dev = seeded (`split.seed` = 1) stratified sample of `split.dev_count` = 20 eligible RTLLM designs by category (Arithmetic / Memory / Control / Miscellaneous, proportional shares, members ordered by a seeded hash of the design_id); held = every other eligible design of rtllm / drrtl / rtlopt / CktEvo set; `cktevo_sky130` = `sky130_subset_count` = 8 set modules with a met sky130hd knee, one per repository first, by size. `scripts/phase1_sets.py` writes the lists into config (rule 4), the designs table (`split`, `tags`) and design.json · PLAN 1.4 / 1.5 · src/designs/sets.py, tests/test_sets_offline.py · Claude Code
- 2026-09-12 · Operational lesson: editing config/experiments.yaml while a batch runs (a) changes `cfg_hash` and therefore the content hash of every later job (cache misses, no correctness issue) and (b) a syntax error in the file makes every runner fail at start (70 trial jobs failed within minutes after a mis-indented insertion; the config-loading tests would have caught it, but the test gate was masked by `| tail`). Rules from now on: validate with `.venv/bin/python -c "from src import config as C; C.load()"` in the same command as any config edit, gate on `${PIPESTATUS[0]}` never on the last pipe stage, and make config edits only between batches · CLAUDE.md working rhythm · scripts/phase1_jobs.py (resubmission of the 71 affected designs) · Claude Code
- 2026-09-12 · Perturbation generator (spec 02 §1) implemented on the Pyverilog AST (`src/noise/`): P1 renames every internal net / register / integer / parameter / genvar (never ports, module, instance, function or task names) with NATO words + index, deterministic per (design, seed, k); P2 permutes the concurrent module items (assign / always / instances) inside each run between declarations and the nonblocking assignments of always blocks that assign distinct registers (both semantics-preserving by construction, no dependence analysis needed); P3 = De Morgan on ~(a&b) / ~(a|b) / !(a&&b) / !(a||b), decimal <-> hexadecimal constants, right-hand-side constant part-selects of width <= 8 -> bit concatenation; P4 = `x <= c ? a : b` <-> if/else and `case` with a default and constant labels -> if chain. Pyverilog re-prints the whole design (comments and layout lost), so the unmodified re-print ("P0 round trip") is written next to the perturbations and SEQ-gated first: a design whose round trip is not proven gets no perturbations. Designs Pyverilog cannot parse (15 of 179 in the sets: multi-target `assign a = x, b = y;`, SystemVerilog `int` loops / unpacked ports / `++`) get no AST perturbations and are listed in reports/data/phase2_perturbations.json · spec 02 §1 · src/noise/vast.py, src/noise/perturb.py, src/noise/generate.py, tests/test_noise_offline.py · Claude Code
- 2026-09-12 · Perturbation gate = the vcf queue kind (V1 -> V2 lock-step -> V3 SEQ, V4 never) per perturbation and round trip, records under results/raw/<design>/EQ/, `perturbations.seq_status` = the stack verdict; only `proven` (SEQ) enters the noise floor (`proven_sim_only`, inconclusive, falsified, rejected are counted and reported as the generator's non-equivalence / unprovability rate). Yosys `equiv_make/equiv_simple/equiv_induct` (`src/equiv/yosys_equiv.py`) is a licence-free self-test of the transforms on synthetic designs only; it cannot pair renamed state and is not a protocol verdict · spec 02 §2, spec 03 · src/noise/gate.py, scripts/phase2_perturb.py · Claude Code

- 2026-09-12 · Lock-step harness sampling (spec 03 §2): inputs change at the negedge and outputs are now sampled just before the following posedge (`#(half − 0.1)`) instead of at the next negedge. Sampling after the posedge made a register inserted in front of a combinational output invisible (vending_machine c2: V2 "identical", SEQ falsified), because the sampling window absorbed the extra register; sampling before the edge follows SEQ's cycle model, so such a candidate shows a constant one-cycle offset (class c2) and V2 offsets mean cycle latency. - 2026-09-14 · **Decisions on the Phase 2 gates G1 / G2 / G3, operations and the C2 redefinition (human; recorded verbatim below, closes reports/phase2.md §6).** Implementation notes follow in later entries · PLAN Phase 2 STOP · docs/spec/02, 03, 04, 05, 07, docs/PLAN.md, docs/PROPOSAL.md, config/experiments.yaml · human

  > Decisions on the Phase 2 gates and the C2 redefinition (record verbatim in docs/DECISIONS.md, dated 2026-09-14; update the specs and config named in each item, re-run `pytest tests/`, then proceed to Phase 3).
  > G1 — noise floor
  >
  > 1. Threshold rule: adopt rule A. t_D = max(2·σ_robust, largest |δ| over D's own proven perturbations including the P0 re-print, pooled q90 of |δ| for that configuration and metric). Store each design's floor class (quiet / spread / offset) next to its floor. Flag any retained gain on an offset design in every table and in the map. Keep σ_robust and the 1σ/2σ/3σ sensitivity in the report. In addition, report every headline number a second time under a fixed materiality threshold (area 1%, power 2%, WNS 1% of the period) as a sensitivity row. Update spec 02 §4, spec 04 §B.2, config `noise`.
  > 2. Designs without a floor: keep them in the search sets with the pooled minimum as floor and a `floor_source = pooled` flag. Exp1 map designs must have a measured floor. For the 14 designs whose perturbations exist but none is proven, add a text-level P1 renamer that bypasses Pyverilog's printer (regex-based identifier renaming with an identifier table from Pyverilog's parse only), so that at least a P1 floor exists. Report the generator's non-equivalence rates per type as a generator-quality metric.
  > 3. Sample size: raise to 8 perturbations per type for the Exp1 map designs and for all spread / offset designs (27), before Phase 4.
  > 4. Rung attribution compares the candidate with D under the same rung and never assumes that a higher rung dominates a lower one. Add this as an explicit test in `tests/` for the diagnoser.
  >
  > G2 — SEQ pilot
  >
  > 1. Class (c2): keep `proven_sim_only` as a separate, weaker evidence class reported apart from `proven` in every table. Attempt a SEQ latency mapping (per-output offsets from V2 fed to the SEQ setup) on the 29 pilot candidates before Phase 5. Class (c2) never enters headline numbers regardless of the outcome.
  > 2. Registers without reset: assume the all-zero initial state for every such register, applied identically to D and to the candidate in both V2 and V3. Record the assumption in spec 03 and in the paper's protocol.
  > 3. V4 stays enabled for clockless modules only, with the three guardrails as recorded.
  >
  > G3 — E4 runtime
  >
  > 1. Screening by a synthesis rung does not enter the main method. `screen.candidates_es = [Y]`. Measure Y's AUROC for E4 retention in Phases 3/4. If AUROC < `screen.auroc_min`, drop the M_noscreen arm and reallocate its budget to starting points. The word "screening" is reserved for synthesis-rung screening. The classifier-based prescreen of decision C2.3 below is a separate mechanism.
  > 2. E4 stays the scoring configuration. H3 stays hidden. Revisit after Exp1 with real candidates.
  >
  > Operations
  >
  > 1. Lock-step VCDs: once a record has SAIF and power ingested with `status = ok`, delete its VCD. Keep VCDs of `sim_fail` and `falsified` records and of a random 2% sample. Future runs write VCDs to scratch and delete them after `vcd2saif`. This follows the existing `dc_work` retention rule: intermediates are deleted, records are append-only.
  > 2. Concurrency: DC and VC Formal are both measured at 50 seats. Set `queue.dc_seats_max = 50` and `queue.vcf_seats_max = 50` as caps, add `queue.*_seats_target` with default 24 for Phases 3–4, and switch the targets to 50 only for bulk Phase 5–6 runs on the user's confirmation. Do not return to 12. License-denial backoff stays on.
  >
  > C2 redefinition (update spec 05, spec 04, spec 03, PLAN Appendix A, PROPOSAL §4.7)
  >
  > 1. C2 is named residual-guided evolution. Mechanisms: (a) fitness = retained gain with the rule-A floor, zero inside the floor; (b) feedback = the synthesizer's verdict (absorbed with capability attribution, duplicate, noise, harmful with locus including `blocks_synthesis`, retained) instead of a scalar; (c) operator selection = bandit initialized from the map prior, credited only for retained or trade-off improvements, credited by the produced class from M6, not the requested class; (d) acceptance = on spread / offset designs, a candidate's gain must exceed the envelope of 2–3 surface perturbations of the candidate itself at E4.
  > 2. The pipeline order stays conventional: V1 interface check → testbench if the design has one → V2 lock-step simulation (SAIF) → V3 SEQ → E4 → diagnosis. "Synthesis-first" is not adopted and is not mentioned in the paper.
  > 3. Prescreen: a candidate whose produced class has map-prior absorption probability ≥ `prescreen.p_min` (initial value 0.9) is not evaluated with probability `1 − prescreen.audit_frac` (audit 10%) and receives immediate feedback with the prior. Add `prescreen` to config.
  > 4. Diagnoser additions: `absorbed_identical` when the E4 fingerprint equals D's (no simulation or proof needed for the verdict, 10% SEQ audit), `duplicate` when the fingerprint equals an earlier candidate's (convergence classes kept per run), `fragile` for the acceptance check in C2.1(d). Add the labels and audit fields to spec 07.
  > 5. Formal proof timeouts: no cutoff discards a candidate. Class-aware safety caps only (initial values: (a)/(b) 1 h, (c1)/(c2)/(d) 4 h), cap → `inconclusive`, candidate kept out of the archive but reported. Report time-to-verdict distributions and inconclusive rates per class.
  > 6. Generations are asynchronous: the next generation is built from the verdicts available, and late verdicts update the archive, bandit credit, and lineage feedback when they arrive. Define the resumption semantics in spec 05 §7.
  > 7. Budget caliber: equal LLM calls is primary. DC hours and VC Formal hours are reported. Efficiency metric = LLM calls per retained candidate. Update `scale.equal_llm_calls_aux` into the main budget rule and keep DC-hour accounting for reporting only.
  > 8. Report the requested-class → produced-class confusion matrix in Phase 3 and Phase 5.
  >
  > Additional tasks before Phase 3
  >
  > 1. Knee sweep extension: for every design whose Φ_main sits at the tightest swept period of a library, add two tighter periods (Nangate45 0.35 and 0.25 ns, ASAP7 0.09 and 0.065 ns, sky130hd 0.9 and 0.65 ns) and recompute Φ_main. Spot-check 5 designs whose Φ_main sits at the loosest period and report whether the cause is the area-jump rule or a timing failure at the next period.
  > 2. `E2r` and `E3` are the same command after the ladder redefinition. Remove `E2r` from every config list to avoid duplicate runs.
  > 3. Hidden-layer coverage check (counts only): for the 99 floored designs, report per configuration H1/H2a/H2b/H3/H5 how many have complete perturbation records and which are missing.
  > 4. Record in DECISIONS as a bounded exception that the E4-vs-H3 agreement statistic in reports/phase2.md §4 was computed from hidden-layer values of baseline perturbations for the G3 decision, that no candidate data was involved, and that no further reads of the hidden database occur before Phase 5 completes.
  > 5. Update `tools.vcformal` seats and STATUS with the measured 50-way parallelism.
  >
  > Deferred to the start of Phase 5
  >
  > 1. Whether H1/H3/H5 run on all E4-evaluated candidates rather than accepted candidates plus a 10% sample.
  >

- 2026-09-14 · **Rule A's pooled minimum is design-weighted (human).** Design principle: the pooled minimum must be insensitive to the number of perturbations each design received; no future change of any design's perturbation count may move another design's floor. Config `noise.pooled_weighting: design` (`src/noise/stats.pooled_quantile`: every design's records share weight 1 / n_d); the record-weighted variant is reported only as a sensitivity row. Trigger: after the spread / offset designs received 8 perturbations per type (up to 33 records per design), the record-weighted pooled q90 of |δ_area| under E4 rose from 0.29 % to 1.43 % (power 1.4 % → 5.9 %) while the design-weighted q90 stayed at 0.28 %, and the re-diagnosis of the calibration candidates under the record-weighted floors turned most trade-offs into retained (luna 57 → 121). The user's reasoning, verbatim:

  > 选 (b)：按设计等权的 pooled q90。理由有三层。
  > 统计上，(a) 是采样设计造成的伪影。 池化最小值的用途是给"安静"设计一个非零地板，代表"表面敏感度在一般设计上长什么样"，因为它们自己的少量扰动可能只是没碰到尾巴。这个统计量应该对"我们决定给每个设计跑几个扰动"不敏感。按记录池化恰恰相反：我们刻意给噪声大的 27 个设计多跑了扰动（每类 8 个），它们的记录就主导了分布，q90 从 0.29% 跳到 1.43% 反映的不是设计总体的性质，而是我们的抽样比例。按设计等权后 q90 仍是 0.28%，说明总体本身没变。以设计为单位加权，才是"总体单位是设计"这个问题的正确估计。
  > 裁决上，(a) 会让适应度出错，而且错在危险的方向。 地板是双侧的：抬高地板不只是把小的正收益判成噪声，也把小的损失判成"在地板内"。校准里 tradeoff 一大半改判为 retained（57 → 121）就是这个效应——一个面积降 4%、功耗升 3% 的候选，在功耗地板 5.9% 之下变成了"纯收益"。这些候选会拿到记功、进入档案，然后在隐藏层的 PTPX 签核下大概率被判投机。也就是说 (a) 会系统性地放大投机率，恰好损害 C3 要证明的东西。
  > 与之前的决定一致。 我们否决过规则 B（池化 q95 = 4.7% 面积 / 10% 功耗），理由是它会吞掉 2–5% 的典型收益；(a) 的功耗最小值 5.9% 已经落到了规则 B 的量级，等于换个名字把 B 请回来。
  > 补三条落实：1. DECISIONS 里把这条写成设计原则：池化最小值必须对每个设计的扰动数量不敏感；今后任何改变扰动数量的操作都不得移动其他设计的地板。2. 报告里两种口径并列给出，加 materiality 行——这正好是一段现成的敏感性分析，说明为什么选 (b)。3. 换到 (b) 之后，把校准候选的裁决分布重算一遍（retained / tradeoff / noise / harmful 各多少），与 (a) 的对照一起存档，后面写论文的 protocol 节要用。

  Implementation: floors recomputed under (b) (visible and hidden); reports/phase2.md §2a shows both pooled q90 columns; reports/phase3.md §5a shows the verdict distribution of the calibration candidates under the stored (run-time) floors, the design-weighted floors and the record-weighted floors, plus the materiality row; the same table is archived in reports/data/phase3_label_sensitivity.json for the paper's protocol section · spec 02 §4 · config/experiments.yaml, src/noise/stats.py, scripts/phase2_noise.py, scripts/hidden_worker.py, scripts/phase3_calibrate.py, tests/test_noise_stats.py · human (decision), Claude Code (implementation)

  >
  > 1. Main model: `llm.selected = gpt-5.6-luna`. Second model: `gpt-5.6-terra`, with an expanded role: terra runs arms M and B2 on all Phase 5 starting points (not a 30-design subset), so that the model-independence check covers the head-to-head comparison in full (budget ≈ 200–250 USD). Ablations run on luna only. gpt-5.4-mini is excluded (31 truncated answers, 7.5 calls per retained candidate). gpt-5.4 is excluded on cost. Update config (`llm.selected`, add `llm.second_model` and its arm list), PLAN Phase 5, PROPOSAL §4.9.
  > 2. Y screen: apply the pre-registered rule now. AUROC 0.748 < `screen.auroc_min` 0.75, so the M_noscreen arm is dropped and its budget goes to starting points. Record the final AUROC when the multi_pipe_8bit verdicts complete, without waiting for it. Keep the Y evaluation of every candidate as a column of the map (cheap, useful for the literature comparison).
  > 3. Floor versioning: adopted. Freeze the floor table at the start of each phase as `floor_version = <phase>`, stamp it on every run and every diagnosis, and never change a run's verdicts after the run starts. Re-diagnosis under another floor table is an offline sensitivity analysis stored under reports/data. Sequence before Phase 4: finish the extra perturbations (G1.3) and the knee-sweep extension, recompute floors once, then freeze `floor_version = phase4`.
  > 4. Recorded: bandit credit and the map prior are indexed by the produced class (M6), and the requested → produced confusion matrix is a reported finding. Add for Phase 5: report B1@E4's produced-class distribution as well, since the static complement prompt is subject to the same weak instruction following, and state this when interpreting M versus B1@E4.
  >
  > Scope decision for C1 (new, record in DECISIONS)
  > C1's claim concerns human-written RTL under full-effort synthesis. RTLLM (textbook reference implementations, largely unoptimized) is outside the scope of the claim: it stays the dev set for calibration, and its results are reported in the paper as an out-of-scope contrast that explains why gains reported on textbook benchmarks are large. Consequences:
  >
  > * Exp1 design set changes from (RTLLM 5 dev + CktEvo 5 held) to CktEvo 5 + Dr.RTL 3 + RTL-OPT 2, all held. Update PLAN 4.1, config `exp1.b0_designs`, PROPOSAL §6.3 and §7.1 wording ("on human-written RTL under full-effort synthesis").
  > * The 10 Exp1 designs are excluded from the Phase 5 starting-point pool (the map prior and the retention predictor are derived from them). Record the exclusion in config `design_sets`.
  > * The C1 claim itself is unchanged.
  >
  > Before Phase 4 (in this order)
  > a. M6 manual validation (PLAN 4.6 moved earlier): stratified sample of at least 60 Phase 3 candidates over produced classes, with emphasis on the (d) versus (a)/(b) boundary and on (c1). The current class distributions (gpt-5.4: 155 of 300 labelled (d); luna: 100 labelled (c1)) suggest that the (d) rule fires on diff size alone. A (d) label must require a change of the operator set or of the dataflow topology, not only a large AST diff. Report rule-vs-human agreement, revise the rules, record in DECISIONS, re-label the Phase 3 candidates.
  > b. Feedback-response metric: replace "children of absorbed parents" (undefined, 0 of 0, because absorbed candidates never become parents) by the `absorbed_identical` rate among children whose lineage feedback contains an absorbed verdict versus children whose lineage does not, per model and per generation. Recompute for Phase 3 and add to reports/phase3.md.
  > c. Ladder runs on the Phase 3 candidates: run E1, E1d, E2, E3, E2g, and the hidden configurations on every E4-evaluated Phase 3 candidate (≈ 800 candidates, ≈ 90 DC hours). These become the RTLLM contrast layer of the map at no LLM cost.
  > d. Extra perturbations (G1.3) for the new Exp1 designs and the 27 spread / offset designs, then knee-sweep extension for designs at the tightest swept period, then floor recomputation and `floor_version = phase4`.
  > e. Phase 5 VC Formal estimate: from the Phase 3 time-to-verdict distributions per class and per design type, project the VC Formal hours of Phase 5 at the planned scale with luna. If the projection exceeds 2 000 hours, propose lowered class caps for arithmetic pipelined designs ((c1)/(d): 2 h) and prioritize the SEQ latency mapping (G2.1(b)) and a DPV phase mapping for fixed-latency arithmetic pipelines. Report the projection in reports/phase3.md as an addendum. No change to the no-discard timeout policy.
  > f. Hidden-layer registration check (counts only): confirm that every accepted Phase 3 candidate is registered for H1–H5 and report per-configuration completion counts.

STOP at G5 with reports/phase4.md.

- 2026-09-14 · **M6 manual validation and classifier rules version 2 (pre-Phase-4 task a; human decision, operator implementation).** 60 Phase 3 candidates sampled over the rules-v1 produced classes (seed 2; quotas d 24, c1 16, a 10, b 10; reports/data/phase3_m6_sample.json, diffs against D in phase3_m6_review.md) were classified by hand under the written protocol of reports/data/phase3_m6_human.json (state-element criterion: (a) same registers, resets and stored values, only the logic re-expressed; (b) register structure or stored state changed without registers crossing logic — block split / merge, encoding, widths, redundant registers, unobservable clears; (c1) registers cross logic at equal latency; (c2) output timing changed; (d) another algorithm, storage organisation or schedule). Human classes: a 3, b 23, c1 9, c2 4, d 21. **Rules v1 agreed on 23 of 60 (38 %); its (d) rule had a precision of 7 / 24 (29 %)**: it fired on any wide text diff (nine coding refactors, four output-timing changes of nonequiv candidates, three retimings and one pure recode were called (d)), it could never reach (d) on a combinational design (the (a) branch came first: 8 of the 10 sampled "(a)" adder_16bit candidates were carry-lookahead / prefix / behavioural `+` adders), and its (c1) rule fired on the 32-bit loop variable of LIFObuffer's reset loop, which the inventory probe counts as a register (57 flip-flop bits before `opt`, 25 after). **Rules v2** (`src/classify/rules.py`, `src/designs/yosys_probe.rtl_stats`, config `classify`, spec 04 §A.1): (d) requires an operator family gained — multiply / divide, add / subtract or variable shift present in C's word-level RTLIL histogram after `proc; flatten; opt` and absent in D's (losses are ordinary recoding; memory cells excluded because Yosys turns small arrays into register lists depending on the indexing style) — or a longest-combinational-path ratio ≥ `classify.d_depth_ratio` = 3 (`ltp -noff`), checked before the structural rules so that combinational designs reach (d); the text diff ratio only flags a rewrite for review; flip-flop bits (including uncollected memories) are counted after `opt`; (c1) requires the flip-flop bits **and** the number of register cells to change (width reductions inside the same cells are (b)); clocked targets include blocking assignments. Outcome on the sample (reports/data/phase3_m6_agreement.json, reports/phase3.md §8): **agreement 40 of 60 (67 %), (d) precision 14 / 14, (d) recall 14 / 21**; (a) 4 / 8 precision, (b) 14 / 16, (c1) 8 / 22, (c2) — (no offsets on nonequiv candidates). The 20 remaining disagreements form four documented groups: output-timing changes of nonequiv candidates (human c2 → rule c1, 4), buffer / schedule re-organisations without operator or depth evidence — shift-register stacks, the valid-bit stack, the FSM-to-phase-counter schedule, radix-4 partial products (human d → rule b / c1 / a, 7), unobservable-state removals and redundant-register removals (human b → rule a / c1, 8), one nonequiv artifact (human a → rule c1). These are the domain of the LLM review of spec 04 A.2 step 2, which is not part of the Phase 3 protocol; the static rules stay explainable and precise on (d). Re-labelling: every Phase 3 candidate is re-classified with rules v2 (`scripts/phase3_calibrate.py m6-relabel`, resumable; `candidates.class_rule_v1` keeps the v1 class, `rules_version` = 2, `features_json` archives the features); the verdict labels, bandit credits and SEQ caps of the runs are run-time facts and stay untouched; the produced-class tables of reports/phase3.md use the v2 classes · PLAN 4.6 (moved before Phase 4), spec 04 §A · src/classify/rules.py, src/designs/yosys_probe.py, config/experiments.yaml (classify), scripts/phase3_calibrate.py, scripts/report_phase.py, tests/test_classify_offline.py · human (task), Claude Code (labels and implementation)

  > Decisions and instructions (2026-09-14, record in DECISIONS)
  >
  > 1. M6 rules v2 accepted. Keep `class_rule_v1` alongside. Add to reports/phase3.md §6a: per-class precision and recall of v2 against the 60 human labels, the four disagreement categories with counts, and the Phase 3 class distribution per model after relabeling (the earlier distribution is superseded). For candidates whose v2 confidence is low or that fall into the four disagreement categories, run the LLM review of spec 04 §A.2 with a documented prompt and store `class_llm` and `class_final`. In the paper, (d) is defined by tool evidence (operator-family addition or a threefold change of the longest combinational path); the recall loss (7 of 21 human-labelled (d) candidates end up as (a)/(b)) is stated as a limitation, and the map's (d) row is read as precision-first.
  > 2. Phase 5 VC Formal projection: accepted as is. 4 121 hours at 50 seats is about 82 hours of wall-clock, which fits the schedule. Do not lower the class caps and do not reduce the share of pipelined designs among the starting points (that would bias the design set). Run Phase 5 proofs in bulk mode at 50 seats. Keep the SEQ latency mapping (G2.1(b)) on the critical path before Phase 5, and implement the DPV phase mapping for fixed-latency arithmetic pipelines only if the latency mapping is done first. Within a generation, submit (c1)/(d) proofs on arithmetic designs before the others so that their tails overlap with the rest of the generation. Record the projection method (per-class, per-design-type time-to-verdict distributions from Phase 3) in reports/phase3.md §5c.
  > 3. Literature objects: 12 non-equivalent under our protocol. Before the re-evaluation table is produced, list the 12 by suite (RTL-OPT / RTLRewriter reference / LLM sample), by verdict (V2 mismatch vs SEQ falsified), and by probable cause (all-zero initial-state assumption, reset polarity, X semantics, port mismatch, or a genuine functional difference), with the counterexample cycle where available. Only proven pairs enter the re-evaluation counts. The non-equivalent ones are reported separately as a benchmark-hygiene finding, with the cause stated. If an RTL-OPT pair fails only because of the initial-state assumption, say so explicitly, since the authors verified those pairs with combinational equivalence.
  > 4. RTLLM contrast layer. The retention rates by class (a 65%, b 62%, c1 90%, d 18%) are reported as the out-of-scope contrast, not as C1 evidence. Add the same table under the materiality thresholds and add the diagnosis-label breakdown per class (retained / trade-off / absorbed_identical / harmful with `blocks_synthesis`). The low (d) retention is worth one sentence of inspection: if most (d) candidates on RTLLM are hand-written arithmetic that displaces DesignWare, that is the first measured instance of `blocks_synthesis` and belongs in the map.
  > 5. Phase 4 go. The ladder and hidden runs on the 116 literature objects may proceed now. B0 candidate generation on the 10 Exp1 designs (CktEvo 5 + Dr.RTL 3 + RTL-OPT 2, all held, excluded from the Phase 5 pool) starts as soon as `floor_version = phase4` is frozen. Everything else per PLAN 4.1–4.9. STOP at G5.
  > 6. Pending closure of Phase 3: after the last multi_pipe_8bit verdicts, run `m6-relabel --force`, append the final AUROC and the redefined feedback-response metric to reports/phase3.md, and mark Phase 3 complete in STATUS.

- 2026-09-14 · **Equivalence-scratch retention tightened under disk pressure (human decision, verbatim: "删除 falsified 的 66.6 GB、压缩 sim_fail 的 18.4 GB、删除非 proven 的 VCS 编译产物 1.9 GB，其余的不动").** Context: the root filesystem was down to 20 GB; results/raw held 1 052 lock-step VCDs (88 GB): falsified 271 files 66.6 GB, sim_fail 372 files 18.4 GB, proven 314 files 2.3 GB (the 2 % sample and records whose power is not yet ingested), inconclusive 21 files 0.1 GB; VCS builds of non-proven records 1.9 GB. Rules from now on (config `retention`): `vcd_keep_verdicts: [sim_fail]` — the VCD of a `falsified` record is deleted (its random-vector simulation passed; the SEQ counterexample file is the diagnostic evidence and stays); `vcd_compress_kept: true` — kept VCDs (sim_fail and the 2 % sample) are stored gzip-compressed (lossless, `.vcd.gz`, the record's `vcd_path` updated, `vcd_compressed` set); `prune_eq_build_nonproven: true` — the VCS build (csrc/, simv, simv.daidir/) of any record that is not proven is scratch. Everything else is untouched (proven-record VCDs under the existing SAIF-and-power rule, inconclusive / rejected VCDs, failed DC runs' scratch, the equivalence records themselves). Applied once to the existing records by `scripts/phase2_saif.py prune` and at record time by `src/equiv/saif.finalize_vcd`; bidirectional tests in tests/test_retention.py · CLAUDE.md rule 5 (approved exception) · config/experiments.yaml, src/equiv/saif.py, src/noise/saif.py, scripts/phase2_saif.py · human (decision), Claude Code (implementation)

- 2026-09-15 · **Diagnoser inputs: two defects found by the independent re-derivation (PLAN 4.6, 09:50).** The manual check tooling re-derives every sampled E4 diagnosis from the raw DC reports (src/analysis/verify.py: area.rpt, qor.rpt, the power reports, timing.rpt and the netlist instances; B.2 re-implemented) — 161 of 165 sampled labels and all gains agreed, and the 4 disagreements exposed: (1) **mixed power bases**: `m3.relative_gains` took `power_saif_mw or power_default_mw` on each side separately, so a candidate with SAIF-based power was compared with a D that only had default-activity power (drrtl_i2c has no SAIF baseline at E4; the literature designs mostly none), producing power gains of +88 % / +93 % out of nothing — 35 of the 213 provisional Phase 4 diagnoses and 178 of the 906 Phase 3 diagnoses were such pairs; (2) **zero band for missing floor components**: `objects.thresholds` returned None for a design without a floor row (RTLRewriter designs) or without a power floor (a D without SAIF), and `diagnose_objects` then dropped all rule-A thresholds and fell back to k_sigma × sigma_robust = 0, so any deviation became a verdict. Fixes (bidirectional tests in tests/test_diagnose_offline.py, tests/test_objects_offline.py, tests/test_verify_offline.py): `m3.power_basis` compares SAIF with SAIF, otherwise default with default, never mixed, and the evidence records `power_basis`; `objects.thresholds` fills a missing component with the pooled minimum of the configuration (the pooled quantile of |delta| stored on every floor row, rule A's third term; G1.2 `pooled_floor_for_missing`) and the diagnosis passes partial thresholds; `phase4_exp1.py diagnose --force` re-derives every proven object (the final chain runs it). Impact: Phase 4 provisional labels 296 → re-derived under the fixed rules on the 373 objects with E4 records (harmful 21, tradeoff 54, absorbed_identical 59, retained 75, absorbed 3, noise 38, duplicate 123) — reported in full after the final run; Phase 3 (search-time diagnoses, kept as the record of what the search saw): re-deriving the 906 E4 diagnoses under the fixed rules changes at most 15 substantive labels (tradeoff → harmful 8, retained → tradeoff 5, harmful → tradeoff 1, tradeoff → retained 1; the other differences are which member of a duplicate group carries the `duplicate` label, an artefact of re-ordering), no zero-band row existed in Phase 3, the per-model retained counts move by at most four in total and the G4 decision stands (addendum in reports/phase3_conclusions.md) · CLAUDE.md rule 2 (both directions), spec 04 B.1 / B.2, DECISIONS 2026-09-14 G1.1 / G1.2 · src/diagnose/m3.py, src/analysis/objects.py, src/analysis/verify.py, scripts/phase4_exp1.py · Claude Code

- 2026-09-15 · **G5 decisions (human; recorded verbatim, dated 2026-09-15, before any Phase 5 work; implementation notes follow in later entries)** · PLAN Phase 5, STOP G5, spec 04, spec 05, PROPOSAL §4.5 · config/experiments.yaml, src/search/, src/equiv/, scripts/, reports/ · human

  > G5 decisions (record verbatim in docs/DECISIONS.md, dated 2026-09-15, before any Phase 5 work)
  > 1. Phase 5: go, with four adjustments.
  >
  > * Starting points: 30 held designs stratified by tier: 18 medium (module-level CktEvo and mid-size Dr.RTL / RTL-OPT designs where B0's proven rate in Phase 4 was non-zero), 6 small human-written, 6 large multi-module. The large tier stays: a zero proven rate there is a result about the LLM and is reported as such. The ten Exp1 designs remain excluded from the pool.
  > * Correctness probe on the three large Exp1 designs (spikeLayer8_H7, drrtl_datapath, drrtl_pcie) with gpt-5.6-terra and gpt-5.6-sol, 2 seeds × K = 6 × N = 5 each, before Phase 5. Decision rule: a model that yields at least 5 proven candidates on a design carries the large tier for arms M and B2 in Phase 5 (equal-call budget unchanged; sol is never the main model). Cost cap for the probe: 120 USD.
  > * Two correctness aids added to the skeleton for every arm, pre-registered: (i) scope-limited rewriting: the prompt names the region to rewrite (the module or always block on the critical path or holding the targeted registers), the answer must return the full file with every other region unchanged, and unchanged regions are verified textually before any tool runs; (ii) one counterexample-guided repair attempt per candidate for V1 rejections, lock-step mismatches (cycle and signals) and SEQ counterexamples, counted as one additional LLM call under the equal-call budget. Report repair yield per failure type.
  > * Phase 5 projections of proofs and calls per retained candidate use the Phase 4 rates by tier.
  >
  > 2. Engineering before Phase 5, in this order: (i) the B1@E4 static complement prompt, written from the literature's own guidance (Dr.RTL's "already done by synthesis" category and the RTL-OPT pattern list), not from our map; (ii) the SEQ latency mapping (G2.1 (b)) so that class (c2) enters the map; (iii) the scope-limited rewriting and the repair step above; (iv) the LLM review of spec 04 A.2 applied only to low-confidence or disagreement-category objects of Phases 4 and 5, prompt recorded; (v) the DPV phase mapping only if time remains. `vcf_seats_target = 50` in bulk mode for Phase 5.
  > 3. Paper form: agreed, with one correction of emphasis. The concentrated map is the central figure of C1. C2 is residual-guided evolution as decided, with the class-prior mechanism demoted to a reported negative result (the class-blind predictor) and the verdict feedback, duplicate / absorbed detection and robustness check as the mechanisms under test. The benchmark-hygiene table and the `add_sub` / `memory_sharing` pair go into the motivation.
  > 4. Additional tasks: (a) re-run the 34 proven RTL-OPT pairs under the authors' published setting (`compile_ultra`, 1 ns, no retime, no gate clock) and report the reconciliation with their 35/36 count; (b) write the class definitions of (b) versus (c1) as used by rules v2 into spec 04 and PROPOSAL §4.5 (latency-preserving refactor with unchanged register count versus with changed register count); (c) inspect the four RTL-OPT divider counterexamples by hand and archive them; (d) report the 128 duplicates by design and by generation.
  > 5. Storage policy for Phase 5. The root partition has 25 GB free and `results/` already holds 83 GB. Before any Phase 5 run: (i) estimate the Phase 5 footprint under the current retention rules; (ii) adopt a tiered retention: full raw artifacts only for accepted candidates and for audit samples; for every other evaluation keep the parsed record, the small reports (qor, area, timing, power) and `meta.json`, and delete netlists, ddc and SAIF after ingest; (iii) report the projected footprint under the tiered policy and the free space required, and stop if it does not fit — the user decides then whether to free `~/.cache` (60 GB of model weights outside the project) or relocate `results/raw` to `/hdd1`. Rule 5 (append-only records) is unchanged: only regenerable artifacts are deleted, never records.

  Accompanying instruction of the same message (human, verbatim): "然后按第 2 节的顺序开始 Phase 5 之前的工程项，并先做第 5 节的磁盘估算——估算结果出来先停下等我决定，其间只做不产生大量磁盘占用的工程项（B1@E4 提示词、SEQ 延迟映射、范围限定改写与修复步骤的实现与测试）。大设计模型探针（第 1 节第二条）和任何批量运行都等我看过磁盘估算后再启动。" Operator reading: the storage estimate of item 5 (i)–(iii) is produced first and reported; until the user's storage decision, only the low-footprint engineering items of item 2 (i)–(iii) run (implementation and offline tests); the correctness probe of item 1 and every batch run wait for that decision.
- 2026-09-15 · **B1@E4 static complement prompt (G5 item 2 (i); operator implementation).** `src/search/prompts/static_complement.md` (version 1; front matter with purpose and sources, stripped before the model sees it) is the fixed block that arm B1@E4 (`search.arms.B1_E4`, feedback `scalar_static`) receives in the cacheable prefix in place of arm M's map-prior table (spec 05 §2). Written from the literature's own guidance and not from this project's map: (A) "the synthesizer already does these" from Dr. RTL's Fig. 7 category "Already done by synthesis tool" (XOR tree balance / chain flatten, bit-level comparison replace), its §1 finding that "manual rebalancing of optimized logic" is absorbed, its released skill library's low-confidence entries (mux-tree restructuring where synthesis already optimizes muxes, wire decomposition solely for hoped timing gain), and RTL-OPT §2 / §2.1 (contrived redundancies are easily optimized; compile_ultra aggressively restructures logic and obscures fine-grained RTL differences); (B) "do this" from RTL-OPT §3.2's six pattern types with their definitions (bit-width optimization, precomputation and LUT conversion, operator strength reduction, control simplification, resource sharing, state encoding optimization) and Dr. RTL's high- / medium-confidence skills (condition pre-computation, one-hot pre-decode, common-subexpression extraction and hoisting, fan-out duplication, carry-select split, borrow lookahead, mux-before-adder, compute-then-select); (C) "do not do" from Dr. RTL's avoid list (latency changes, counter direction, priority flattening without exclusivity, width narrowing, algebraic regrouping, LFSR, memory addressing) and RTL-OPT §4's three LLM failure modes. The test asserts that the body carries no map prior, no Experiment-1 rates and no percentage at all; `runs.prompt_version` of a B1 run ends in `+sc<version>`; arms M / B2 / B0 never see the block (bidirectional test in tests/test_search_offline.py). The papers' text was read from arXiv (2604.14989, 2601.01765) and the staged Dr_RTL skill library · spec 05 §2 / §5, PLAN Appendix A · src/search/prompts/static_complement.md, src/search/prompts.py, src/search/driver.py, tests/test_search_offline.py · Claude Code
- 2026-09-15 · **SEQ latency mapping implemented (G2.1 (b), G5 item 2 (ii); operator implementation, first real run).** A candidate whose lock-step simulation finds constant per-output offsets (V2 `offset`, class c2) no longer stops at `proven_sim_only`: with config `equiv.seq_latency_mapping: true` V3 runs the project's SEQ script with `map_by_name -input` (inputs by name only) and one `seq_assert spec.<o> impl.<o>` per output, `-clock spec.<clk> -latency1 0 -latency2 k` for an output the candidate delays by k cycles (VC Formal's latency semantics: the later signal is compared with `$past` of the earlier one by the difference after `##max`; the user guide's example `-latency1 {10 0 9} -latency2 {4 5 9}` gives `##10 (spec === $past(impl, 6))`), immediate assertions for k = 0 outputs, so every output stays compared. SEQ's own verdict then decides (`decide(..., latency_mapped=True)`: proven / falsified / inconclusive; a mismatch is never mapped away); `equiv.json` records `latency_mapped` and `v3.latency`; the record hash gains `latency_mapping: true` only while the switch is on, so every earlier record keeps its directory. Parser: with explicit output assertions VC Formal Y-2026 prints the assertion counts under "Property Summary: SEQ" instead of "Sequential Equivalence Summary: SEQ"; the project parser (`seq_tcl.parse_seq_log`) accepts both (the flow's regexes are reused unchanged, `flow/` untouched). Population rule (spec 03 §2, config `equiv.c2_population: false`): a latency-mapped candidate is evaluated at E4 and diagnosed — it enters the map's (c2) row — but is never archived, accepted or credited until the protocol-recognition rules exist; `equiv.c2_headline` stays false. Verified both directions with the real tools (tests/test_equiv_eda.py): the pilot's counter_12 candidate that registers `out` once more (offset {out: 1}, `proven_sim_only` in the Phase 2 pilot) is **proven** under the mapping in 30 s, **falsified** when asserted at the wrong offset 2, and falsified under the plain by-name mapping; offline tests cover the script lines, the switch in both positions, the verdict rules, the hash and the driver's population rule. The pilot re-run (`phase2_pilot.py latency --submit`: 28 class-(c2) candidates × 2 seeds = 56 VC Formal jobs, config PILOT_LAT; `latency-collect` writes reports/data/phase2_pilot_latency.json and reports/phase2.md gains §4b) is prepared and waits for the user's storage decision (batch runs on hold) · DECISIONS 2026-09-14 G2.1, spec 03 §1 / §2, G5 item 2 (ii) · config/experiments.yaml (equiv), src/equiv/seq_tcl.py, src/equiv/seq.py, src/equiv/stack.py, src/equiv/verdict.py, src/equiv/run_equiv.py, src/search/driver.py, scripts/phase2_pilot.py, scripts/report_phase.py, tests/ · Claude Code
- 2026-09-15 · **Scope-limited rewriting and the counterexample-guided repair call (G5 item 1, correctness aids; G5 item 2 (iii); operator implementation, offline tests).** (i) Scope: `src/search/scope.py` scans comment-stripped Verilog into statement-level items per module (always / initial blocks with their assignment targets, continuous assigns, instantiations with the instance table, functions, tasks, generate and specify blocks; declarations are free) — checked on all 593 staged modules (5 855 items, no overlap). The region of a call comes from the critical endpoints of the parent's fitness evaluation (or D's E4 baseline): on a multi-module design the module reached through the instance path of the top-k endpoints (`exp5.correctness_aids.scope.endpoints` = 3; e.g. eth_txethmac → `eth_txcounters`, drrtl_i2c → `i2c_master_bit_ctrl`), on a single-module design the always block(s) assigning the endpoint registers (`_reg` suffix and indices stripped; e.g. fsm_encode → the block assigning reg1); tool-generated names (mc_obct_top's retimed R_2) or missing path data fall back to the whole module with the reason recorded. The suffix names the region ("rewrite only ..."; every other always block, assign, instantiation, function and task must be returned textually unchanged; declarations may change); before any tool runs the answer is checked item by item (whitespace and comments never count; a changed, removed or moved item outside the region is a violation): a violating answer is labelled `scope_violation` (candidate and diagnosis rows — the diagnoses CHECK gained the label, older databases are rebuilt —, `scope_json` with the region and the first violations, feedback block, credit 0, the call spent, no equivalence job, no repair); an accepted answer carries the region in `scope_json`. (ii) Repair: after a `rejected` (V1 detail), `sim_fail` (first mismatch cycle, outputs, original vs rewrite values) or `falsified` verdict (falsified outputs and the shortest counterexample depth, now parsed from `report_fv -verbose` into `v3.cex_depths`) the driver makes one repair call (`prompts.repair_suffix`: the failed RTL, the evidence, the same class instruction and scope); the answer is a new candidate with `repair_of` = the failed one, the same parent and requested class, and goes through the whole pipeline (scope check included); a repair's own failure, an inconclusive or error verdict, an unusable answer and an exhausted budget get no repair (skips counted in the run state); the call is charged to the run's equal-call budget, so the next generation has one call fewer (`exp5.correctness_aids.repair_attempts` = 1, `repair_failure_types`). Repair yield per failure type: `src/analysis/repair.py` (attempted / proven / accepted / retained-or-improved / scope violations per original verdict, plus the unrepaired failures) for reports/phase5.md. Both aids are on for every arm by config and can be switched off (tests cover both positions); the Phase 4 generations test now sees one repair call on its falsified candidate · G5 decisions item 1, spec 05 §1 / §2 · src/search/scope.py, src/search/driver.py, src/search/prompts.py, src/equiv/seq.py, src/analysis/repair.py, src/db/schema.sql, src/db/core.py, config/experiments.yaml (exp5.correctness_aids), tests/test_scope_offline.py, tests/test_search_offline.py, tests/test_db_migration_offline.py, tests/test_equiv_offline.py · Claude Code
- 2026-09-15 · **G5 additional tasks (b), (c), (d) done (operator; no runs).** (b) The (b) versus (c1) definition as rules v2 apply it is written into spec 04 §A.1 and PROPOSAL §4.5: both latency-preserving; (b) keeps the number of register cells (state re-encoded, widened, narrowed, cleared differently or moved between blocks, no register crossing logic), (c1) changes it (registers cross logic, are duplicated, merged or removed; flip-flop bits and cell count both differ, counted by Yosys after `proc; flatten; opt`); a width change inside the same cells is (b). (c) The four RTL-OPT divider references were inspected by hand (reports/data/phase4_divider_counterexamples.md): non-restoring (original) versus restoring (reference) division; the pairs differ **only** on division by zero with the dividend's MSB set (`result` 2^n-2 versus 2^n-1, `odd` equal) and agree for every non-zero divisor — the three V2 traces show exactly that pattern and a directed Icarus simulation confirms it for all four widths (64/64 differ with B = 0 and the MSB set, 0/64 with the MSB clear, 0/4 000 with B ≠ 0); the 32-bit pair's SEQ counterexample (3 071 s) is the same corner, which 20 000 random cycles miss at P(B = 0) = 2^-16. Verdicts unchanged (rule 8); the hygiene cause in the paper reads "division by zero: quotient differs when the dividend's MSB is set; equivalent for every non-zero divisor". (d) `phase4_exp1.py duplicates` writes reports/data/phase4_duplicates.json (duplicates by design, by generation, by design × generation, the generation gap to the repeated answer, and identical rewrites across runs of one design by content hash) and reports/phase4.md gains §4c: 128 of 1 473 answers repeat an earlier answer of their run (B0 126, literature 2) · G5 decisions item 4 · docs/spec/04-classifier-diagnoser.md, docs/PROPOSAL.md, reports/data/phase4_divider_counterexamples.md, scripts/phase4_exp1.py, scripts/report_phase.py, reports/phase4.md, tests/test_phase4_offline.py · Claude Code

- 2026-09-15 · **Decisions on the storage estimate, the Phase 5 scenarios, the pilot re-run, the sol prices, the approved re-runs and the pre-probe review (human; recorded verbatim; implementation notes follow in later entries)** · G5 item 5, PLAN Phase 5 · config/experiments.yaml, src/eval/retention.py, src/equiv/saif.py, scripts/ · human

  > Decisions (record in DECISIONS, 2026-09-15)
  >
  > 1. Storage: option A, with one amendment to the 2026-09-14 VCD decision. Run `python3 scripts/prune_scratch.py --tiered --apply`. Amendment: for `sim_fail` records keep the VCD of a random 5% sample (seeded) instead of all of them; the mismatch cycle and signal values already stored in the database are the evidence the repair step and the paper use. Everything else in the tiered policy stands (accepted candidates, audit samples, literature objects and failed records untouched; records and database rows never deleted). Report free space after the prune. Option C is not needed. Option B (`~/.cache`) is the user's own call and will be answered separately.
  > 2. Phase 5 scenarios: enable "H1/H3/H5 on all E4-evaluated candidates" now (+6 GB). Enable "ladder re-run for accepted candidates" only if the user frees `~/.cache` (option B); otherwise defer it to Phase 6.

- 2026-09-15 · **RTL-OPT pairs under the authors' published setting (decision 2026-09-15 item 5, G5 item 4 (a); reports/phase4.md §4d, reports/data/phase4_rtlopt_setting.json).** 68 DC runs (34 D baselines, 34 references) under `E2_1ns` (compile_ultra, 1 ns, no retime, no gate clock, DesignWare, this project's SDC convention, DC W-2024.09): of the 33 evaluated proven pairs **13 are better by area, 4 same, 16 worse** (the mux_dead reference does not link, §4a); at the knee period under E2 / E4 the same pairs give nearly the same split. The paper's Table 1 reports 35 of 36 better under compile_ultra at 1 ns; that count is not reproduced by our flow. The authors' released DC reports (data/sources/RTL-OPT/Results/RTL-OPT_DC) come from a different setting — their run_dc.tcl and command.log show plain `compile` at CLOCK_PERIOD 0.1 ns with `set_max_delay` from all inputs to all outputs, `set_transform_for_retiming dont_retime`, register merging / sequential area recovery / clock gating through hierarchy off, `ungroup -all -flatten`, DC T-2022.03-SP2, their own Nangate45 typical.db — and under it every released pair (40 of 40, 34 of our 34) is better by area, with D areas two to four times ours (comparator_16bit 244.5 vs 88.3, decoder_8bit 856.0 vs 246.8): a plain compile at a tight clock keeps the suboptimal version's structure, compile_ultra removes most of it. Differences left for a future isolating run of their exact script with compile_ultra and 1 ns (68 DC runs): DC version, their input-to-output max-delay constraints versus this project's I/O delays, register merging and sequential area recovery switched off. For the paper: the RTL-OPT "35 of 36" is a property of the compile / tight-clock setting and of an area-and-PPA judgement, not of compile_ultra at 1 ns as reproduced here; under this project's protocol 11 of the 34 proven pairs retain at E4 (§4) · decision 2026-09-15 item 5 · config/experiments.yaml (E2_1ns), scripts/phase4_exp1.py, scripts/report_phase.py, reports/phase4.md · Claude Code

  > 4. RTL-OPT reconciliation: one more run, then the wording. Paper wording: state the settings found in the released artifacts and our observations under each setting; do not characterize the authors' claim beyond what the artifacts show.   > 5. Large-design correctness probe: go. `python3 scripts/phase5_probe.py create --submit` (12 runs, arm M, K 6 × N 5, cap 120 USD at the live prices). Condition: the pre-launch report shows every projection inside its cap (LLM USD ≤ `budget_usd.phase5_main`; disk ≤ free space at launch minus 20 GB; DC and VC Formal hours as projected). If any projection is outside its cap, or the probe yields zero proven candidates for both models on all three designs, stop and report instead.
  > 7. Records. Keep the pilot result (26 of 28 (c2) candidates proven under the latency mapping, 2 falsified because the simulated offset relation does not hold in the reachable state space) for the paper as the argument for formal over simulation-only evidence. Keep the LLM-review counts and the unchanged map shape in reports/phase4.md.

(2) **Static complement text version 1 approved** (item 2), unchanged. (3) **Prefix fixes** (item 3): (i) `search_system_y.md` opens with the tool-neutral objective ("so that logic synthesis produces a smaller, faster or lower-power netlist"); the Y-caliber summary carries only what Yosys + OpenSTA measured (numbers, cell mix, critical endpoints) and no DesignWare or DC-log line; the scalar arms B0 / B2 carry neither the map-prior sentence nor the static block; test: B0's prefix contains none of Synopsys / Design Compiler / compile_ultra / -retime / -gate_clock / DesignWare / E4 / map prior / static guidance, while the same design's M prefix does. (ii) `prompts.synth_log_line`: the E4 summary's synthesizer line is the log-category counts plus the normalised DC message types (identifier × count, e.g. OPT-776 x4) and the integrated-clock-gating / datapath / register counts — never a raw message or a file path (the earlier line printed the whole log-summary dict including sample messages with record paths); identical for M, B1@E4 and B2 (test). (4) **`E1_authors`** (item 4): config `E1_authors` = plain `compile` at 0.1 ns with `set_max_delay -from [all_inputs] -to [all_outputs] 0.1`, `set_transform_for_retiming [get_cells *] dont_retime`, `compile_enable_register_merging false`, `compile_sequential_area_recovery false`, `set_fix_multiple_port_nets`, `ungroup -all -flatten`, standard synthetic library (the settings of the released run_dc.tcl / command.log on DC W-2024.09; this project's SDC adds its I/O delays); `phase4_exp1.py rtlopt-setting --config E1_authors --submit`: 68 DC jobs submitted (34 baselines, 34 references); the collector now writes every reproduced setting side by side with the authors' released reports, the report's §4d states the settings found in the artifacts and the observations under each reproduction without characterising the authors' claim, and PROPOSAL §3.2's settings table gains the released-artifact row · decisions 2026-09-15 items 1–4 · config/experiments.yaml, src/eval/retention.py, src/search/driver.py, src/search/prompts.py, src/search/prompts/search_system_y.md, scripts/status.py, scripts/phase4_exp1.py, scripts/report_phase.py, docs/PROPOSAL.md, tests/ · Claude Code
- 2026-09-15 · **Probe launched, stopped after 10 minutes, and the scope aid amended (operator decision, reported to the user; the probe restarted with the amendment).** `phase5_probe.py create --submit` at 17:20 (12 runs, arm M, terra and sol on spikeLayer8_H7 / drrtl_datapath / drrtl_pcie). After 140 calls (5.5 USD) the runs had produced 74 answers and **not one candidate reached the equivalence stack**: 41 of 41 issued candidates were `scope_violation` ("module missing from the answer" ×5–6 on every one) and 55 answers were unusable ("the rewrite does not declare module top (found ['descrambler'])" on pcie). Cause: the three probe designs are multi-module, the scope block named one module ("rewrite only module X … return the complete file(s) with every other module textually unchanged") and both models returned that module alone — on pcie the region module is not the top, so the answer failed the top-module check before the scope check. The pre-registered aid's purpose (unchanged regions verified textually before any tool runs) is intact under the amendment; only the demand to echo the unchanged modules is dropped: (1) `scope.splice`: for a module-level region, every module of D that the answer does not contain is taken verbatim from D's text and the full file is reconstructed (the answer must contain the region module; block-level regions and complete answers are untouched); the candidate record carries `scope_json.spliced`; the top-module check runs on the spliced file; any other module the answer *does* return is still verified unchanged. (2) The scope block now says: "Return the rewritten module in full (you may return only this module: every module you do not return is taken unchanged from the original; any other module you do return must be textually unchanged)". (3) Bidirectional tests (scope and driver: region-only answers are spliced and issued, an answer that alters the top module outside the region is still a violation, block-level regions unchanged). (4) The 12 runs of the first launch were stopped (their queue jobs killed and marked failed with exhausted attempts, `runs.status = superseded`; a retried job of a superseded run now exits at once) and count 6.7 USD against the probe cap; 12 new runs follow with the same seeds. Recorded here as a deviation from the letter of decision 2026-09-15 item 1 (i) of the G5 decisions; the alternative — letting the probe finish with zero evaluated candidates — would have triggered the "stop and report" clause of the launch decision on a formatting artefact rather than on the models' correctness · G5 item 1 (i), decisions 2026-09-15 items 5–6 · src/search/scope.py, src/search/driver.py, scripts/phase5_probe.py, tests/ · Claude Code

The arm keeps B2's fitness and archive; its prompts follow Dr. RTL: the optimizer / timing-analyzer role as system text, the released skill library in the prefix, per call the K worst paths of the parent's E4 timing report with slack and the critical path's cell chain plus a rotating diversity strategy (no class instruction), and the run's learned skills; after every built round one skill-extraction call (group-relative summary of the round → at most three pattern–strategy entries with a confidence tier) is charged to the equal-call budget before the next round's calls are counted (the last round is never distilled; no call beyond the budget). Dr. RTL's timing score is not used as fitness (WNS ≈ 0 at the knee makes its normalisation undefined; the arms are compared under the same three-component gain). Spec 05 §5 records the implementation. The Phase 5 plan now includes the arm; the pre-authorised launch takes the plan as it stands when the probe finishes · PLAN 5.2, spec 05 §5 · config/experiments.yaml, src/search/prompts/search_system_drrtl.md, src/search/prompts.py, src/search/driver.py, docs/spec/05-search.md, tests/ · Claude Code

- 2026-09-15 · **Search runs get their own queue pool (`queue.search_max: 16`; operator scheduling fix before the launch, tests).** A search run (kind `search`) polls for the queue jobs it submitted; arm B0's fitness runs (kind `yosys`) go to the local pool, the same pool that held the search runs (16 seats). At the Phase 5 scale (630 runs queued at once, 90 of them B0, the large tier first) 16 B0 runs holding every local seat would wait forever for yosys jobs that can never be dispatched; Phase 3 / 4 never had 16 B0 runs at once. Now `POOL_OF_KIND["search"] = "search"` with cap `queue.search_max` = 16 — the same number of concurrent runs the local pool bounded them to before, so no concurrency cap was raised (rule 6) and `local_max` stays 16 for yosys / sim / llm / shell jobs.   > Decisions (2026-09-15 evening, record in DECISIONS)
  >
  > 1. Hold the launch. The watchdog must not run `phase5_main.py launch` until items 2–4 below are done and reported. The pre-authorization and the caps stand unchanged afterwards.
  > 2. Block-level scope: splice, do not discard. For single-module designs, treat an out-of-scope edit the way multi-module splicing already does: keep the model's rewrite of the scoped block, restore every other block from D textually, then run the pipeline. Record `scope_violation` as a warning flag on the candidate, not as a discard. If the spliced file fails V1, the existing one repair call applies. Report the block-level violation rate from the two M smokes and from the first Phase 5 generation, per arm. Pre-register this in DECISIONS as an amendment to `correctness_aids.scope`.
  > 3. Model assignment by tier (amends the G5 rule). Large tier (6 designs): every arm (B0, B1_E4, B2, M, DrRTL_reimpl) runs with gpt-5.6-terra, so that the within-tier comparison is single-model; luna additionally runs arm M on the large tier (3 seeds) as the model-correctness contrast, reported apart from the main table. Small and medium tiers: luna for every arm, terra for M and B2 (model-independence check). Sol is not used in Phase 5. Update the plan, recompute the pre-launch projections (calls, USD, disk, DC and VC Formal hours) and report them; the caps stand.
  > 4. Register the probe candidates in the hidden loop (H1/H3/H5 on all E4-evaluated probe candidates, H2a/H2b/H4 on accepted and audit samples), and keep spikeLayer8_H7 in the large tier: its near-zero proven rate under both models is the LLM-correctness limit and is reported as such.
  > 5. Equivalence stack frozen for Phase 5. Set `equiv_version = phase5` (V1, testbench, V2 with the all-zero initial state, SEQ with the latency mapping for measured offsets, V4 for clockless modules only) and stamp it on every Phase 5 verdict. The DPV phase mapping is deferred to Phase 6; no change to the stack during Phase 5.
  > 6. Retention for H4. Ensure the tiered retention keeps the E4 netlist of every accepted, archived or audit-sample candidate until its H4 record exists; record the 13 Phase 4 candidates whose netlists were pruned before H4 as `skipped` in the hidden coverage table.
  > 7. Launch condition after 2–5: the same as before — every projection inside its cap, then GO without further approval; report the launch summary (runs per arm and model, seat targets, projected USD, hours, disk) when the watchdog fires.

- 2026-09-16 · **Phase 5 concurrency: `queue.search_max` 16 → 40 with a VC Formal backpressure rule (user decision of 2026-09-16, "改到 40，加背压规则"; operator implementation, tests 379 passed).** `search_max` counts the concurrently running search runs (light processes: LLM calls, in-process classification, job submission and polling; no licence); the VC Formal and DC seat caps (50 / 50) are unchanged. `queue.backpressure.search = {pool: vcf, max_waiting: 50}`: the daemon starts no new search run while the vcf pool runs at its limit and more than 50 proofs wait, so verdicts keep arriving within a generation's wait (`search.gen_wait_sec` 30 min) and the evolution's feedback does not lag; seats left idle by the per-design fairness cap do not count as saturation. Expected effect (estimate): the large tier ≈ 12 h instead of ≈ 31 h, the small tier ≈ 5 h instead of ≈ 12 h, the medium tier stays bounded by its ≈ 1 900 proof seat-hours (≈ 38–45 h at 50 seats): Phase 5 ≈ 2.5 days instead of ≈ 3.7. Behaviour from now on: `llm.QuotaExhausted` (a 429 carrying insufficient_quota / credit_balance_exhausted is not retried) makes `run_search` mark the run `paused_quota` and exit 75, the queue's temporary-failure code — the pool backs off and retries without consuming an attempt, so runs resume by themselves once the account is topped up. Restart procedure after the top-up: `queue.search_max` back to 40, `scripts/queue/daemon.py stop` / `start`, resubmit the runs whose job rows are `failed` (`Queue.submit("search", {"run_id": ...})`), then `phase5_main.py status` · config/experiments.yaml, src/jobqueue/core.py, src/search/llm.py, src/search/run_search.py, tests/ · Claude Code

- 2026-09-16 15:0x · **User: operational scheduling change (verbatim; recorded before implementation; no change to verdict definitions, floors, budgets or `equiv_version`):**
  > Operational scheduling change (record in DECISIONS with timestamp; no change to verdict definitions, floors, budgets or `equiv_version`)
  >
  > 1. Submit E4 for every candidate as soon as it passes V1/V2, in parallel with its SEQ job, and compute a provisional diagnosis from the E4 result.
  > 2. Order the VC Formal queue by provisional diagnosis: retained / tradeoff first, then noise / harmful, then absorbed / absorbed_identical / duplicate last. Every candidate still receives its proof under the frozen stack; only the order changes. The archive admits proven candidates only.
  > 3. Provisional diagnoses may be fed back to the LLM before the proof completes, flagged "equivalence pending"; late verdicts update the archive, credit and lineage as the async design already allows. Apply identically to every arm.
  > 4. Hidden-layer registrations pause while a tier's search runs are active and resume in bulk when the tier finishes (they are off the critical path).
  > 5. Report, 15 minutes after the change, the solver CPU share and the queue composition, and re-estimate the A / C report times.

  Context: at 14:23 the host ran at load 129 (50 hidden DC registrations + 50 proofs), 74 % of the VC Formal seat-hours ended inconclusive at the a / b caps, and the operator had just capped hidden DC jobs at 8 (previous entry) · human
- 2026-09-16 15:14 · **Operational scheduling change implemented (operator; the user's five items above; offline tests both directions; daemon restarted and the running search runs rolled onto the new driver at 15:14).** (1) *Split pipeline* (config `search.early_fitness.enabled`, default on): a candidate's equivalence job is now a `sim` job (V1 + V2 only, local pool, kind `sim`, timeout `timeouts.sim` + 5 min); when V2 passes, the fitness job (E4, or Y for B0) and the proof job start together — the proof is a `vcf` job whose payload carries `sim_record`; `stack.check_equivalence(sim_record=...)` copies the sim record's stages (ports, control ports, lock-step result, SAIFs) and runs V3 (V4 where the stack allows) only; its record hashes under stage set `v3` (`sim` jobs under `v1v2`, the one-job pipeline under `full`), and a decided `full` record of the same pair from an earlier run (proven / falsified / rejected / sim_fail) is reused instead of proving the same RTL twice. A sim record that decides by itself (rejected, sim_fail, or `proven_sim_only` for an offset pair without the mapping) ends the candidate as before (nonequiv + the repair call, or the fitness job alone). The row's `eq_job_id` becomes the proof job's id (the job whose time the analysis reads; the sim job's id stays in the run state), the V1 / V2 columns are written after the sim job and the verdict stays NULL until the proof; `equiv_version` is stamped on the verdict as before. Retention: both records are slimmed under the same tiered policy once the candidate is final and its fitness job has ended. DC-hour consequence: fitness evaluations now also run for candidates whose proof later fails (charged to the run's `spent_dc_hours`; identifiable as `e4_job_id IS NOT NULL AND verdict != 'proven'`). (2) *Proof ordering*: config `search.early_fitness.priority_by_label` (retained / tradeoff / improved +4; noise / harmful / no_gain / fragile +2; absorbed / absorbed_identical / duplicate +0; `priority_undiagnosed` +2 until the fitness result arrives), added to `search.job_priority` (4) and the `long_proof_first` boost; `Queue.reprioritize` re-orders the queued proof (a running proof keeps its seat); the queue's order stays priority DESC, then submission time, round-robin across designs within a level. Every candidate still receives its proof under the frozen stack; the archive admits proven candidates only. (3) *Provisional feedback*: when the fitness result precedes the proof, the driver computes the diagnosis the final rule would give (M3 with the same thresholds and floor for arm M, the scalar block for B0 / B1@E4 / B2 / DrRTL — no envelope, no fingerprint registration, no credit, no archive, no `diagnoses` row) and puts it in the lineage feedback with `"equivalence": "pending"`; the prompt suffix (every arm, incl. the Dr. RTL suffix) adds one sentence explaining the flag whenever a block carries it; `candidates.note` gets `[provisional <label>: equivalence pending]`; the proof's verdict then replaces the block with the final diagnosis on the same fitness record (envelope when required) or with nonequiv (+ the repair call). Generation cadence unchanged (`gen_wait_sec` 1800 or every candidate final). (4) *Hidden registrations*: `hidden_loop.eligible_designs` registers only the designs of tiers whose planned runs are all done (`hidden_worker.py --submit-candidates --design ...`); the probe experiment is not gated; the 357 hidden DC jobs already queued at 15:0x keep running under the 8-job ceiling of the previous entry (not withdrawn — the user's call). (5) The 15-minute report follows in STATUS. *Rolling running runs*: SIGUSR1 makes the new driver leave after the step in progress (state saved) with exit 76, an old driver (no handler) dies with 128 + 10; the queue requeues both as resumptions without consuming an attempt (`EX_RESTART`, `ROLL_SIGNAL_RC`); the roll script signals a driver only while it sleeps between steps (wchan `hrtimer_nanosleep`). *Dispatcher defect found on the way*: while backpressure held fresh runs the dispatcher scanned only the first `free` rows, so a resumption queued behind them was never reached — the B0 / terra / seed 1 run of spikeNeuron8_H7, failed at the credit outage and requeued at 14:3x, sat unstarted for an hour; fixed (whole-queue scan while the hold is on; test). Reversal: `search.early_fitness.enabled: false` restores the one-job pipeline for new candidates; candidates in flight finish on the path they started · src/search/driver.py, src/equiv/stack.py, src/equiv/run_equiv.py, src/jobqueue/core.py, src/search/run_search.py, src/search/prompts.py, scripts/hidden_loop.py, scripts/hidden_worker.py, config/experiments.yaml

No record, budget or verdict touched · jobs table

- 2026-09-18 05:35 · **Defect: daemon restarts re-spawned running search runs (stale done marker after a code roll) — 30 runs got a second driver, 15 show damage; fixed (operator).** Mechanism: a code roll (SIGUSR1 → exit 76) requeues the job under the same attempt number, so the shell wrapper's `<job>.a0.done` marker ("76") stays on disk while the restarted attempt runs; `Queue._recover` read the marker before checking the process, treated the running job as finished, requeued it without an attempt and started a second driver on the same run. It happened at the restarts of 2026-09-17 14:06 (2 large-tier runs, both ended clean: 60 calls, no lost candidate), 2026-09-18 05:09 (28 medium-tier runs) and 05:32 (16 of the same again → three drivers). Two drivers on one run make LLM calls independently and overwrite each other's state file: 15 runs show it — budget-ledger calls above the 60-call caliber (up to 71), candidates aborted as orphans (up to 10 per run), and 6 runs in status `failed` after the drivers' collisions (sub_32bit / B2 / terra, calculation / B2 / terra and luna, router / B2 / terra, UART / B2 / terra and luna, vga_wb_slave / B2 / terra); the other 15 re-spawned runs show no anomaly (ledger = state calls, no aborted candidate) and are watched. At 05:35 the 34 orphan drivers were stopped (SIGTERM; the daemon-tracked driver of each job kept; 29 drivers = 29 running jobs afterwards). Fix: `_recover` adopts a job whose process is alive and consults the marker only once the process is gone (and only a marker newer than the job's start); `_spawn` removes a marker of the same attempt before starting; `_alive` treats a zombie as dead; tests both directions. Remedy for the 15 damaged runs — proposed to the user: mark them `superseded` and re-run them fresh under the same (model, arm, design, seed) (the equal-call caliber is broken for them; ≈ 15 × 1–2 USD), keep the 15 clean ones · src/jobqueue/core.py, docs/DECISIONS.md

  >    a. At most 24 runs generating at once; admission order unchanged (least-run-row-first across rows, window and lanes as configured).
  >    b.   >    c. Report hourly: runs generating / waiting / queued, the unverified-at-build fraction per arm-model row, and the estimated proof-queue wait per lane. These join the §7c verification-conditions table.
  >    d. Reversible: if the unverified-at-build fraction of any arm-model row stays above 35 % for three consecutive hours, revert to counting waiting runs and report.
  > 2. simple_spi stays in the long-pole lane; the window keeps the max(16, …) rule for the remaining cheap designs.
  > 3. (i) item 3, outstanding: report for SPI and simple_spi the seat-hours per run and the inconclusive share per class measured on harness_version 2 proofs only, against the v1 figures (SPI 54.4 h/run, 85 %); state whether the 01:54 rebalance used v2 or v1 figures for SPI and recompute the ETA if it used v1.

Guardrails: `queue.generating_max: 24`; `queue.admission_guard {unverified_at_build_max: 0.35, proof_wait_max_min: 60, window_min: 60, tier: medium}` — the daemon computes, every dispatch tick, the medium tier's unverified-at-build fraction over the generations built in the last hour (gen_summary.pending_json against the previous generation) and pauses every admission while it exceeds 35 % (logged on each change), and it skips a fresh run whose lane's estimated proof-queue wait (queued proofs × mean proof minutes of the last 6 h / seats; the window designs as one group) exceeds 60 minutes; `queue.count_waiting_runs: true` restores the old accounting (the 1d revert). The same figures join §7c of every report. Tests: both directions (a waiting run frees a slot; generating_max caps; the fraction guard pauses and resumes; the lane-wait guard skips the lane's run while a window run passes; the revert switch restores the old count).
